#7096. Yeah, I’ll Try the Red-Light Therapy Vibrator

It’s been a big few years for red-light therapy. You can get it in a skin-clearing LED face mask, a hair-growth-stimulating cap, or a sauna blanket you can self-zip into like a burrito, and I’ve cheered on every new format. I just assumed we’d eventually run out of places for it. Oh, but reader, turns out there was one place left.

LELO, the Swedish luxury sex toy brand, just put red LEDs inside a G-spot vibrator called the MONA Spectra, and the brand says you can get the benefits of red-light therapy (collagen! circulation!) along with, you know, the main event.

As someone who reads the ingredient list on a $14 serum before it gets anywhere near my face, I had questions. So yes, I tried the red-light therapy vibrator. Here's my honest review, plus what the science actually says about red-light therapy for your vagina.

At its core, the MONA Spectra is a G-spot vibrator. It borrows the curved silhouette of LELO's longtime MONA line, with a rounded tip angled toward the front wall of the vagina (aka G-spot territory) and soft, body-safe silicone wrapped in twisted ribs for extra texture. It has 10 vibration settings and 16 intensity levels, and it's fully waterproof, so the bath is fair game.

The twist (besides the literal twisted ribs) is a built-in array of red LEDs. Out of the box, it runs red light at 615 to 625 nanometers, and the LELO app unlocks near-infrared, blue and green light, plus extra vibration patterns.

If you've ever bought an LED face mask, you already know the pitch. Red light is thought to help your cells repair themselves, which is why it's become such a skin-care staple, and LELO is applying the same idea to vaginal tissue.

LELO says regular use may support collagen and elastin, tissue elasticity, natural lubrication, and sensitivity. The brand also says the LED array gives off a gentle warmth that can relax the pelvic floor muscles and increase blood flow, which, for the record, your body tends to appreciate during sex anyway.

The pitch, basically, is folding a wellness habit into something you might already be doing.

The short answer: it's promising, but it's early. Most of the research on red light and vaginal health has focused on menopause, when dropping estrogen can lead to dryness and thinner, more sensitive tissue. A 2019 review of the research so far concluded that red-light therapy appears to be safe and may help with those symptoms, but the authors called the evidence preliminary, and none of it was testing an at-home vibrator.

LELO ran its own four-week survey with 37 women, and nearly 8 in 10 of those using it a few times a week reported noticeably more natural moisture, while close to 70% said they felt more confident in their bodies. It's an encouraging start, and bigger studies will tell us more. Until then, LELO itself positions the MONA Spectra as an everyday self-care device rather than a medical one, which is the right way to think about it.

So for now, think of the red light as a nice extra. If you're dealing with dryness or pain during sex, talk to your OB-GYN, who can help you figure out what will actually make a difference.

If you've used a LELO vibrator before, the MONA Spectra will feel familiar. It has the same gently curved shape and soft, smooth silicone as the regular MONA, and the twisted ribs add a little extra texture. The vibrations are what you'd expect from LELO, with enough settings to keep things low-key or turn them all the way up.

The one difference is warmth: with the LEDs on, the toy gets a little warm, which LELO's instructions say is normal.

If you're buying it for the red-light benefits, give it time. LELO's own survey ran for four weeks, and the brand recommends sticking to 15 minutes of light a day.

Yes (which makes the price easier to swallow). The lights are optional, so if the red light ends up being the thing you skip, you still own a high-end G-spot vibrator, and a very good one at that.

From what researchers have seen so far, yes, as long as you're using a device designed for intimate use and following its instructions. The MONA Spectra checks those boxes: it's made of body-safe silicone that meets ISO 3533, the international safety standard for sex toys, and LELO says its LEDs are UV-free, so you're not exposing sensitive skin to the kind of light that causes damage. A few ground rules from LELO:

If you're pregnant or managing a health condition, check with your doctor before adding any light therapy device to your routine, and read LELO's user manual for the full list of warnings.

If you already have a red-light routine and you've been eyeing a nicer G-spot vibrator anyway, the MONA Spectra makes a lot of sense. If you mostly want a great G-spot vibe and the light therapy is a maybe, LELO's regular MONA has the same familiar shape for a lot less money.

Yes. LELO says it's 100% waterproof and safe for the bath or shower.

LELO says the app is anonymous and fully encrypted, and you don't need it to use the toy's basic settings.

$299 is a lot of money for a vibrator, no way around it. It's also in the same price range as plenty of red-light face masks, and none of those double as a sex toy. The MONA Spectra is a seriously good G-spot vibrator, and the red light is a fun bonus for anyone who's already bought into the wellness of it all. If you haven't, stick with a regular LELO vibrator and save the money.

#7094. AI Leaders Warn of Risks at United Nations Security Council Briefing

The heads of OpenAI, Anthropic, and Hugging Face recently joined AI researcher Yoshua Bengio at the United Nations headquarters in New York on September 23 to warn the UN Security Council about the security risks of increasingly capable AI.

They did not offer a single plan for managing those risks. Bengio called for licensing and independent safety reviews. OpenAI's Sam Altman proposed common standards that countries could adopt through their own laws. Anthropic's Dario Amodei urged AI developers to slow down when safety work cannot keep pace. Hugging Face's Clément Delangue warned that limiting access to capable systems could leave defenders at a disadvantage.

France, which held the Security Council's rotating presidency, convened the meeting on AI and international security. The discussion exposed a difficult question for governments: how to oversee technology that crosses borders when countries disagree about who should set the rules.

Bengio, co-chair of the UN's Independent International Scientific Panel on AI, challenged the idea that competition forces companies to keep building more powerful systems. "The race is not a law of nature: it is the product of choices," he said in his published remarks. "They've told us they would slow down if they could. They can."

He called on developers to show independent experts that frontier systems are safe to train and deploy. He also proposed incident reporting, licensing, and liability insurance. Bengio identified catastrophic misuse, concentrated power, and the loss of human control as risks that require international cooperation.

Altman acknowledged the danger of moving too quickly. "We have unilaterally slowed down in the past. We will do so in the future," he said, according to remarks published by OpenAI. Decisions about AI's direction "cannot be made by labs in San Francisco alone," he added.

Altman proposed complementary national and international standards to measure frontier AI capabilities, assess risks, and test safeguards. He said countries should be able to compare evidence and verify commitments. He also called for faster reporting of serious incidents and secure channels for sharing emerging threats. Such standards should accommodate both open and closed models without favoring established companies, he said.

Amodei made a more direct commitment about the pace of Anthropic's releases: "We will slow down as much as necessary," he told the council. He identified two principal concerns: the misuse of AI to develop biological weapons and the possibility that advanced systems could outstrip developers' ability to control them.

The Anthropic chief proposed placing outside evaluators inside AI companies with access comparable to employees. He suggested that governments begin with a narrow agreement against using AI to make biological weapons, then develop ways to evaluate models and verify commitments across borders. He also called for common testing standards and a system for notifying other countries of significant AI incidents.

Delangue drew on a July incident in which Hugging Face disclosed an AI agent cyberattack. He called for stronger monitoring and disclosure, but argued that safety also depends on giving defenders access to capable tools. The central danger, he said, was "asymmetry of powerful AI" between attackers and defenders, large companies and everyone else, and wealthy countries and the rest of the world. His team used an open-source model while responding to the attack, he said.

The U.S. government opposed a broader international regulatory role. White House science adviser Michael Kratsios told the council that countries should develop their own ability to use and govern the technology. In the U.S. statement, he rejected new global governance structures and used the phrase "superintelligence, formerly known as AI," adopting President Donald Trump's terminology from his General Assembly speech the previous day.

The four AI briefers differed on how far oversight should go and how widely capable systems should be available. They agreed on a narrower point: Governments need better ways to understand what advanced AI systems can do and to detect when safeguards fail.

John K. Waters is the editor in chief of a number of Converge360.com sites, with a focus on high-end development, AI and future tech. He's been writing about cutting-edge technologies and culture of Silicon Valley for more than two decades, and he's written more than a dozen books. He also co-scripted the documentary film Silicon Valley: A 100 Year Renaissance, which aired on PBS.  He can be reached at [email protected].

Technology initiatives, curriculum mandates, instructional frameworks, new programs, new platforms, new expectations, and professional development. Every year — and sometimes every month — something else is added to teachers’ plates. The result can be fragmentation, burnout, and a lack of focus.

#7091. The 2026 Esquire Cologne Awards

Every product was carefully curated by an Esquire editor. We may earn a commission from these links. Here’s how we test products and why you should trust us.

Whether you’re looking to make a big impression on a special occasion or just searching for a great everyday scent, these winners have you covered.

There’s never been a better time to be a guy who wants to smell good. The men’s fragrance market is booming, the scents are bolder than ever, and you can shop for pretty much anything you could possibly imagine—from long-lasting extracts for a big night out to easy-to-wear options that’ll fit right in at the office—with ease.

The only problem with all that abundance is the project of narrowing things down to a manageable number of scents that might work for you. Which is where we come in. This year, the team here at Esquire decided to go big for our annual Cologne Awards. Our fragrance experts called in hundreds of new releases over the course of the year, narrowing them down through extensive testing and internal debate before each creating their own short list of winners.

That’s when things got really interesting. Round by round, the Esquire Cologne Awards committee met to discuss potential winners, spritzing and testing again and again before finally producing the list you see here. These are the 40 best scents of the year. You might not connect with every one of them—but if you can’t find something you love here, well, good luck out there.

This has become one of our daily wears—and for good reason. It’s going to please pretty much anyone. Spicy and aromatic, this cologne is vanilla and amber forward with discernible notes of Iris germanica and lavender giving it a more floral edge. The result is sensual and soft but never unmasculine, thanks to the aromatic freshness. Plus, it comes packaged in a giant flask. There’s nothing manlier than having a giant flask (literally) of cologne behind your bathroom mirror.

Leather lovers, this one is for you. Tom Ford’s newest Ombré Leather Reserve blends notes of earthy, black leather with silver juniper and sandalwood, creating a balanced, rooted, yet refreshing take on Ford’s classic Ombré Leather eau de parfum. Inspired by the great American West and the mountains and deserts that constitute it, this scent is a perfect balance between refinement and rugged masculinity.

If you were around in the ’90s and spent some time at the mall, you probably remember Gap Dream. It was the most iconic scent of the original lineup, which was discontinued in the early aughts. But Gap has given the people what they want: a relaunch of its beloved fragrance line. Dream remains the hero of the collection, fresh and clean like a brand-new white T-shirt, with bright citrus, soft florals, and botanical musk.

Noble but knows how to fuck. A raw oud with fresh sensuality. The peach is jam-like, adding a softness to the suede accord. Velvet Mood is a leather fragrance with added warmth, texture, and juicy sweetness. (It recalls the intimate, sexual encounter in Call Me by Your Name. The one with the peach. You know it.) It’s captivating and aura-maxxing, not a blind buy for most. But we’re still on the side of biting the peach.

This cologne has one job, and that job is beach. It’s salty, woody, and as fresh as anything else we smelled this year. From the blond driftwood to the beach rose, coconut water, and sea salt, all the notes are reminiscent of tropical paradise. It may not be summer anymore, but the perfect summer scent has landed. Now that it’s in your possession, you can turn any day into a beach day.

Though it is totally within the Ranger Station wheelhouse of western-inspired cowpoke smells, it’s a new earthy floral. The easy choice would have been to mix sandalwood and ambergris with whiskey and call it another saloon smell. Instead the Ranger Station team gave it an iris root heart—soft and floral without losing its edge. It’s not betting the farm and drinking away the loss. It’s betting the farm, losing it, and stepping out those double swing doors to see a sunrise and smell a new dewy and green morning. You lost it all, but that was yesterday. Keep moving forward and the next win can’t be too far down the road.

It took four years to get right and was, according to the company, the last thing on which Giorgio Armani put his personal stamp of approval. And it’s not what you might expect if you’re thinking of the aquatic, mineral freshness of something like Acqua di Giò. I Will is a different animal—all wood and vanilla with bergamot to keep things from getting too dark and dense. It’s rich and modern and exactly what people will be talking about 20 years from now when they’re discussing the Italian fashion house’s other iconic scents.

Soft and sensual, Musc Pallida lingers like a whisper on your skin. Powdery iris and warm musk are the main components here, and they work together seamlessly. It’s light and airy but doesn’t fade quickly, and it smells good on pretty much anyone. Don’t go into Musc Pallida expecting your typical manly musk fragrance—it’s much more elegant and intimate.

Earthy sweetness, earthly delights. This fragrance from Byredo is sweet without ever venturing into gourmand territory. Even with its Madagascar vanilla and vanilla liquor accord at the base, it is punctuated with fougères and florals that give it complexity without steering it into being a spice bomb or a cognac cocktail. Instead it has a bright greenness and soft musk that can see the light of day and what’s to come. Simply fresh, refined with some sweetness—for a beautiful day.

A sophisticated blend of cinnamon, sage, and bergamot gives this fragrance a warm, spicy character. Cinnamon fragrances tend to be overpowering and reserved for nighttime, but this scent feels sophisticated and is a great daytime spice since the spicy top notes are balanced by a vanilla and praline base. It’s an elegant take on a spice-forward scent and a Nordstrom exclusive, which makes sense since Parfums de Marly is one of the retailer’s top-selling brands.

Creed’s Iris Debonair—a totally new offering, not a new expression—walks a wonderful line. It’s very gentlemanly with its cool and classic fougère heart. The opening and its transition into the middle is a lot of bergamot, absinthe, and lavender, making it seem like an expected (albeit exceptional) barbershop scent. But as it settles, iris starts to come to the forefront. It’s not the old-fashioned iris of Green Irish Tweed but a softer one that pairs well with a soft ambrette dry down. It has a true masculine and a true feminine side, and neither controls your lasting memory of the scent. Totally captivating.

Citrus and amber are not natural bedfellows, but this scent—a more highly concentrated version of the former Le Gemme Tygar—makes a very strong case for their marriage. It’s bright and grapefruit-y in one instant and then rich and woody in another. The more you smell it, the more the moniker starts to make sense. It is, after all, named after tigereye, a gemstone revered not for its sameness but for the striking impression that its contrasting bands of color create.

Fig fragrances are having a moment, but this new Granado EDP blows all other fig scents out of the water. Candied green fig reveals rich, jammy notes that are sweet but not in the typical syrupy way; it’s a lush, even fresh kind of sweet. Once you get hints of sweet dates, florals, and woods, it all combines to create a beautifully creamy gourmand.

It opens fruity and floral. A burst of raspberry gives way to peony, geranium, jasmine, magnolia. That might sound a little tough for some guys—but remember it’s just the beginning. Those notes soon give way to a base of deep, powdery musks. It’s not your typical masculine fragrance; it’s something a little more delicate and, though the scent is rooted in centuries of Chinese tradition, modern. We’d encourage you to give it a try. You might just find a new daily driver.

Before you spritz this woody scent, you have to admire the artistry of the bottle. Mind Games is known for its chess-inspired bottles, and Knight’s Tour is a great example of that. The scent inside is a sophisticated blend of lavender and saffron with spicy accents up top and smooth woods at the base. It’s a great go-to option for every day.

One Esquire editor made Ripe her scent of the summer after it launched in the spring. The reason? Its perfect balance of warm florals, fresh fruity notes, soft woods, and musks is lush and enticing. Have you ever thought about how notes of lychee, watermelon sorbet, green leaves, rose water, and creamy sandalwood work together? Spoiler alert: It’s pretty awesome.

Ojai is like waking up in a luxury hotel in California, sunlight peeking through the shades as fruity and floral notes dance around the room. Juicy, ripe mango and honey-like saffron are at the heart of this fragrance, giving it a sweet yet elevated quality. But it also has an earthy, amber-y depth to it, thanks to patchouli and frankincense at the base.

Put rhubarb in a fragrance and we’re there. Demon Dancer takes the tartness of rhubarb and blackcurrant and adds bright citrus, fresh peppermint, and radiant orange blossom for an exciting opening before it settles into a woody base of amber, musk, and tonka bean. When the cool, fresh top and heart notes reveal the warm base, it really is something to take in.

Imagine that feeling when you’re walking around a smog-choked city and find an unexpected oasis. It’s fresh, green, but with just that tiniest hint of something darker and deeper. That’s this scent. It’s wood and pepper and greenery and a reminder that even in the midst of urban sprawl, we can always find a way back to nature.

The Dolce & Gabbana classic is now available as an alcohol-free perfume gel, making it a travel-friendly option for sensitive skin. The curved metal applicator makes it easy to apply the fragrance directly to pulse points, while the pocket-sized packaging is perfect for on-the-go touch-ups. The gel formula also works as a great base for layering if you already own the original fragrance and want to extend the wear time of the cult-favorite citrus scent.

#7089. Mark Rober's Class CrunchLabs Courses Now Available for Free on YouTube

Class CrunchLabs, the suite of free curriculum resources from NASA engineer turned YouTube science communicator Mark Rober, has released its first two courses on YouTube.

Designed in partnership with the National Science Teaching Association, the standards-aligned "Carnival of Collisions: Contact Forces" and "Mission MagLev: Electric & Magentic Forces" courses offer free video lessons, guided activities, and supplemental teaching resources for grades 6-8.

Class CrunchLabs was launched last year to offer free hands-on science curriculum resources including teaching guides, classroom slides, student readings and handouts, assessments, and instructional videos featuring Rober alongside "Science Bob" Pflugfelder, celebrity guests, and student co-hosts. The initiative was backed by a $7.2 million grant to create the CrunchLabs.org Foundation, a nonprofit "dedicated to revolutionizing science education in a way that promotes critical thinking and inspires lasting curiosity and confidence in STEM," according to a news announcement at the time.

The organization plans to roll out additional courses on YouTube over time, with the ultimate goal of posting more than 1,000 science videos in 34 languages.

Check out the Class CrunchLabs YouTube channel here.

Rhea Kelly is editor in chief for Campus Technology, THE Journal, and Spaces4Learning. She can be reached at [email protected].

#7087. Patrick Stewart Was a Serious Watch Guy. Here Are 5 of the Late Hollywood Legend's Best.

From a treasured Rolex to his beloved IWC, the late actor had a lifelong appreciation for the art of watchmaking.

“Someone once told me that time was a predator that stalked us all our lives," begins Captain Jean-Luc Picard, played by Sir Patrick Stewart in Star Trek Generations, in one of his most memorable lines. "I rather believe that time is a companion who goes with us on a journey, reminds us to cherish every moment, because it will never come again.”

Those musings on time feel particularly poignant now, following the British actor's death. There’s something rather comforting about the idea of time as a companion, particularly when you consider Stewart’s lifelong fascination with the very objects we use to measure it.

Because, as it turns out, Patrick Stewart was quite the watch guy. Not simply someone who happened to wear an expensive timepiece on the red carpet, but a genuine enthusiast with an appreciation for the craftsmanship, history, and ingenuity behind them. He bought his first watch at 18 and, by his own admission, never threw one away.

“The watch industry never ceases to amaze me,” he told the Fondation de la Haute Horlogerie in 2019. “Because a watch is more than just an object that gives the time. Behind every watch there is an idea, a design, a concept, an innovation, and the watchmaker's desire to create something that is greater than anything that has ever existed before.”

It's an enthusiasm that stayed with him throughout his life, from the Rolex he reportedly spent almost all his savings on after arriving in Hollywood to his enduring affection for IWC Schaffhausen. And, naturally, there were some rather lovely watches along the way.

So, in celebration of an extraordinary career and a life well lived, we've taken a look back at some of Patrick Stewart's finest watch moments.

Perhaps the most meaningful watch in Stewart's story is one for which we don't have an exact reference. Long before he became one of Hollywood's most recognizable actors, Stewart was given a rather unexpected piece of career advice by the actress Eva Marie Saint. She advised him that appearances mattered, and that an expensive watch might help convince producers he was already a successful actor. So, she took him to a Beverly Hills jeweller, where he picked out a Rolex. The only problem? It cost him his entire savings. “I was eating spaghetti and boiled potatoes for months after that!” he recalled in a 2019 interview with the Fondation de la Haute Horlogerie.

It's touching anecdote given Stewart's modest upbringing. Having grown up in poverty, he admitted that it took him years to feel comfortable spending money on beautiful things. But eventually, he came to realize there was nothing wrong with enjoying the rewards of his success.

Tissot Le Locle Powermatic 80 to the 2017 Canadian Grand Prix

One of Stewart's most entertaining watch moments came at the 2017 Canadian Grand Prix, when he joined Daniel Ricciardo on the podium and celebrated the Australian driver's third-place finish by drinking champagne from his racing boot. Yes, really.

And while most people were understandably distracted by the sight of Captain Picard downing champagne from a sweaty shoe, there was a rather lovely watch on his wrist, a Tissot Le Locle Powermatic 80. The reasonably priced Swiss automatic features a silver guilloché dial, Roman numerals, a black leather strap, and an impressive 80-hour power reserve. Proof that even a relatively affordable dress watch can look the part.

Long before his appearances at watchmaking galas, Stewart was already making a convincing case for the understated dress watch. In this archival portrait, the actor wears a small, gold-tone, rectangular timepiece, seemingly a Cartier Tank, on a dark leather strap. Paired with a pale linen-style suit and a knitted white top, it's a particularly handsome combination.

First introduced in 1917, the Cartier Tank has become one of watchmaking's most enduring designs, worn by everyone from Andy Warhol to Muhammad Ali. Its appeal lies in its simplicity: a rectangular case, elegant proportions, and a design that has barely needed changing in more than a century. And judging by this photograph, Stewart understood the appeal of a good dress watch long before his later affection for IWC became public knowledge.

IWC Portofino Automatic to the 2018 Academy Awards

When it came to dressing for the red carpet, Stewart understood the appeal of keeping things classic. At the 90th Academy Awards in 2018, he opted for an IWC Portofino Automatic, reference IW356504, a rather beautiful dress watch that proved you don't need an enormous, diamond-covered timepiece to make an impression. Housed in a 40mm case of 18-carat rose gold, the watch features a silver-plated dial, slim gold hour markers, and a discreet date window at three o'clock. It's finished with a dark brown alligator leather strap and powered by an automatic movement. There's nothing particularly flashy about it, which is precisely what makes it so appealing.

IWC Portugieser Chronograph Classic at the 2017 SIHH Gala

By 2017, Stewart's appreciation for fine watchmaking had brought him into the company of some of the industry's most celebrated names. That January, he attended IWC Schaffhausen's Decoding the Beauty of Time gala in Geneva alongside his wife, Sunny Ozell, sporting a rather handsome IWC Portugieser Chronograph Classic.

First introduced in 2013, the reference 3904 is a particularly elegant piece of watchmaking. Featuring a 42mm case, distinctive Arabic numerals, and a railway-track minute scale, it's a slightly sportier proposition than the Portofino. Inside sits IWC's automatic calibre 89361, complete with a flyback chronograph function and an impressive 68-hour power reserve. The evening celebrated the Swiss manufacturer's renewed Da Vinci collection, but Stewart's choice of wristwear showcased another of IWC's most recognizable designs. And given that he once named IWC as his favorite watchmaker, it's hardly surprising that he had such good taste.

#7085. 7 Peter Millar Staples That Work Wherever the Day Takes You

Every product was carefully curated by an Esquire editor. We may earn a commission from these links. Here’s how we test products and why you should trust us.

Good clothes, fewer decisions. What’s not to like?

Getting dressed for just about anything is a whole lot easier when you have the right essentials in your closet. Whether you’re heading into the office, catching a flight, or packing for a weekend away, a few well-chosen pieces can take you from one plan to the next without having to give your outfit much thought.

That’s the appeal of Peter Millar’s Crown Collection, which brings together classic menswear with thoughtful details, quality fabrics, and a relaxed approach to dressing well. There’s an emphasis on comfort and practicality, but never at the expense of looking polished. From tailored trousers and half-zips to quilted layers and other everyday favorites, these are pieces you'll find yourself reaching for again and again.

Ahead, seven picks from the collection, available at Bloomingdale’s, that deserve a spot in your closet. Because looking good shouldn’t require a whole lot of effort.

A chore coat is a great casual Friday-to-weekend staple when you want to look put together without feeling too dressed up. This navy style has a relaxed, cardigan-like feel, with a classic collar and patch pockets that work just as well with a T-shirt and jeans as they do with a button-down and chinos.

The Essex Vest is an iconic piece within Peter Millar’s Crown Collection and is designed as a versatile layering option for fall that works across a range of occasions. This black diamond-quilted style offers an extra layer of warmth without the bulk of a jacket, making it just as useful for your morning commute as it is for a round of golf or an afternoon apple picking.

Few pieces strike the balance between comfort and looking sharp quite like a good half-zip. This navy pullover is comfortable enough for a long flight but polished enough to wear over a collared shirt with tailored trousers. Prediction: You’ll want it in multiple colors.

A quilted coat is timeless, especially in a rich, dark olive shade. This one has a classic countryside feel, but its clean shape looks just as at home on city streets with jeans and boots or layered over a button-down and trousers. A bottle opener tucked inside the left interior pocket adds a handy signature touch. It’s a smart choice for chilly days that don’t call for a heavy overcoat.

Utilitarian pants are a wardrobe icon, and this five-pocket design offers a slightly more refined take on the classic. The smooth sateen fabric and rich blue color give them a polished look that works with everything from a polo at the office to a crisp shirt and loafers for dinner.

Every man’s shoe collection should include a pair of driving loafers, particularly when comfort and looking elegant are equally important. These walnut-brown suede drivers feature classic penny-loafer detailing and flexible, rubber-studded soles, making them a natural choice with chinos, tailored trousers, or jeans.

A checked button-down is a classic for a reason, but how about one with a little more personality? This soft twill style features a small-scale pattern and subtle grapeseed color that look particularly great with navy, gray, or khaki. Designed to be tucked in, it pairs easily with tailored trousers for work or sateen five pocket pants for a more relaxed look.

#7084. These Beauty Gift Sets Are, IMO, Far Better Than Advent Calendars

I have a personal vendetta against those advent calendars, so I set out to find better options.

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

Beauty advent calendars are, in my humble opinion, one of the worst things to ever happen to holiday gifting. I know that by writing this story I am likely to be labeled a hateful killjoy, but I simply cannot stay silent any longer.

I’ve been a beauty editor for over 15 years and, for all 15 of those years, my guilt about the amount of trash this industry produces follows me around like a lipstick-covered napkin I can’t kick off the bottom of my shoe. The vast majority of beauty products aren’t recyclable because they’re too small, contain too many different materials, or are made from a type of material municipal recycling facilities just don’t accept. The tiny percentage of products that are refillable aren’t always actually helping sustainability efforts either.

Beauty advent calendars, while absolutely cute and fun and nostalgic and great for content, combine all of these wasteful nightmares into one. All the advent calendars I’ve encountered are hiding mostly miniature products behind those tiny cardboard doors, and the box itself is usually covered in festive, gilded designs that all but guarantee the outer packaging will have to be diverted into the trash too.

The only truly good part of beauty advent calendars I can see is that they do bring some whimsy to this often hellish timeline. I get why that’s so tempting! But I suspected we could do better and still have plenty of holiday fun, so I set out to find beauty gift sets that were even better than advent calendars.

My three guiding principles for making this list were:

This list is pretty short, because it turns out a lot of gift sets are kinda lame: The products tend to be smaller than usual (which, again, means there’s no chance they’ll be recycled) and/or the savings aren’t significant enough to justify all the extra packaging. Still, I managed to dig up 10 beauty gift sets that hit all the points above.

Now this is what I mean by useful gifts: In addition to a full-size Loose Ends Eau de Parfum, this Noyz set includes two faux leather coasters and an ice cube tray. Everyone needs coasters! And everyone needs a tray for big ice cubes, even if they don’t realize how life-changing it will be yet. The brand doesn’t sell the bonus gifts on their own so it’s not clear how much they’re worth, but the price ($85) is how much the EDP usually costs on its own, so you’re certainly getting more bang for your buck. Loose Ends is a lovely winter scent too: It’s warm and boozy, with notes of vanilla, burnt sugar, and cognac.

If waste is your primary concern with advent calendars, this is probably the best set on the list to mitigate that. These are solid body lotions (meaning they eventually disappear completely after you use them) housed in small cardboard boxes (one of the easier materials to recycle curbside). Each stone is usually $38 each, so you’re getting a 20% discount by buying the set—perfect for people who are already fans of the brand and want to stock up for the year.

I had to triple check that the price was right on this set, since the full-size Delina perfume alone is usually only $75 less than the total here. Add the usually $90 hair perfume and usually $105 body cream and you’re talking about saving $120 when you buy this kit. If you’re more of a fan of citrusy Valaya than floral Delina, there’s the same set for that scent too.

Is dry shampoo the sexiest gift in the world? No. But buying them in a set of two saves you almost 30%, so this is ideal for anyone you already know is a Batiste devotee (me, for example). The limited edition holiday fragrance, Berry Disco, also makes it feel a little more special for gifting season.

Sometimes, the best gift sets aren’t holiday gift sets at all. The Gel Mani System from Olive & June isn’t a seasonal special, but it’s a great buy for anyone whose 2027 resolutions involve doing their own nails at home. You’re saving a bunch of money by buying the set (which includes an LED lamp, multiple gel polishes, and 9 tools you need for a DIY set) instead of the separate products and saving in perpetuity since the whole thing costs less than two gel manicures. Plus, it all comes in a cute little box so you don’t even have to wrap it up.

Another kit that’s more about stocking up on the basics than anything super flashy, but well worth the savings since the set is the same price as just the full-size cream. If you were intrigued by Cosmo contributing writer Catharine Malzahn’s rave review of the Kiehl’s Ultra Facial Cream, ‘tis the season to give it a try.

Here’s another fragrance set with a non-beauty gift a reasonable person would actually use. Once again, this whole kit is the cost of a single full-size fragrance (which is also included) but you get a travel size and sleek white candle as well. Somewhere but Nowhere is, IMO, a crowd-pleasing gourmand, with notes of cedarwood, vanilla cream, and cardamom.

Jones Road has a highly devoted following—you likely already know who on your gifting list is obsessed with the brand. In addition to offering up solid savings (you basically get the lip gloss for free), this Pinky Bronze is a shade you can only buy via this set, so it feels extra special. And that minimal, cardboard packaging?! I’m swooning.

Lancôme does have an advent calendar, but I think this beauty box is the better deal. For one, it comes in a pretty red and pink travel case a human would actually use. For two, you get seven full size makeup and skin care products, plus four generous travel sizes. The price of the entire kit is 35% cheaper than the full-size serum alone. There is one catch: This is a gift with purchase, so you have to buy a separate full-price Lancôme product in order to get all this stuff for $89. Still, that’s a sweet deal for all my Lancôme heads out there.

I love that every soap in this eight piece set could be a gift on its own, thanks to the pretty paper wrapping on each bar. Again, I love a product that eventually disappears completely. If you’re a fan of Beekman 1802’s soaps, this is definitely the time to stock up: You save more than $50 by getting them in this set.

Kara McGrath is a contributing beauty editor for Cosmo with over 15 years of experience reporting on the beauty industry. Her inbox currently contains pitches for hundreds of gift sets.

#7083. OpenAI Getting Rid of Custom GPTs and Moving to Plugin Architecture

OpenAI has announced it will retire Custom GPTs and move the specialized workflows built around them to a new plugin architecture spanning ChatGPT and its Codex coding agent.

Custom GPT retirement is currently scheduled for Dec. 11, 2026, although the company says timing and migration availability can vary by account and workspace.

For affected Enterprise workspaces, OpenAI's current migration schedule targets Oct. 26 for ending creation of new Custom GPTs. Qualifying Enterprise customers with approved deferrals can continue using them until Feb. 11, 2027.

OpenAI has changed its UI to make GPTs less prominent, and when users choose to try creating a new Custom GPT, they are now prompted with an option to create a plugin instead.

Rather than maintaining separate customized versions of ChatGPT, OpenAI is shifting customization toward plugins that can package reusable instructions, reference material, and connections to outside services.

Under OpenAI's plugin architecture, a plugin can include one or more skills containing instructions and resources for repeatable workflows, a Model Context Protocol (MCP) server exposing tools and external systems, or both. ChatGPT and Codex share a universal plugin directory, although some capabilities can remain specific to a particular environment.

For organizations, that makes the change more than a simple renaming exercise. A company could package recurring workflows into skills while using MCP servers or connected apps to give those workflows access to enterprise systems and data.

OpenAI says creators will be able to migrate an existing GPT through the My GPTs interface. The GPT's instructions become a skill in the replacement plugin, while knowledge files are copied into plugin reference files and connected apps can be carried over.

Not everything transfers automatically. Existing GPT conversations do not migrate, the selected model does not carry over, and Custom Actions may have to be rebuilt using an available app or a custom MCP server.

Sharing also changes. Migrated plugins start private, existing GPT users do not automatically receive access to the replacement, and prior sharing settings do not carry over.

OpenAI is also cautioning organizations against assuming that migration will preserve behavior exactly. "A migrated plugin may respond differently," the company says, recommending that users retest familiar prompts, reference-material use, required output formats, and integrations before relying on the replacement.

That warning has contributed to pushback from some GPT builders, who argue that plugins do not fully reproduce the dedicated and predictable environment of a Custom GPT. Other complaints have focused on sharing, migration complexity, and differences in how migrated workflows use instructions and reference material.

Despite those concerns, OpenAI is clearly consolidating around plugins as its common mechanism for extending its AI products. Its developer documentation now describes plugins as packages users can "discover, install, share, and publish" across ChatGPT and Codex, replacing the separate-custom-assistant model with reusable AI capabilities that can be combined with enterprise tools and services.

For more information, go to the OpenAI site.

David Ramel is an editor and writer at Converge 360.

#7077. AI Ambitions Are Running Into a Network Infrastructure Trust Problem

Enterprise ambitions around AI are running into a fundamental problem: Orgnanizations aren't always confident in the networks those projects depend on, according to a report from telecommunications provider Arelion.

The research found that concerns about network providers have caused organizations to rethink strategic initiatives over the past two years. Among respondents whose initiatives were affected, AI and data-driven projects were the most cited, at 49%. That put AI ahead of security and compliance programs at 44%, digital transformation at 42%, and cloud migration at 38%.

The findings come from Arelion's report, "The Trust Illusion: Why AI and Digital Transformation Need More Than a Reliable Network," based on a survey of 518 senior enterprise network decision-makers in the U.S., U.K., Germany, and France conducted by Savanta.

All respondents worked for organizations with more than 2,000 employees and influenced network strategy across data centers, cloud, and connectivity. Of those surveyed, 66% had final sign-off responsibility. The results suggest that as organizations pursue increasingly data-intensive AI projects, confidence in the network infrastructure supporting them could become another deployment obstacle.

On the surface, Arelion found little evidence of a trust crisis. A total of 93% of respondents said they largely or completely trust their current network provider, while 83% said that trust has strengthened over time.

The picture changes when respondents are asked about a serious network incident. Only 15% said they have complete confidence in their provider's ability to quickly detect and address such a problem. Another 42% said their provider seriously lets them down anywhere from a few times a year to monthly or more frequently.

Those concerns have consequences beyond the network itself. Responding organizations have delayed, scaled back, or stopped strategic initiatives because of provider trust concerns, while others have added resiliency or risk-mitigation measures.

AI and data projects were the most affected among respondents reporting an impact, as organizations try to move AI from experimentation into larger production deployments.

A serious provider failure could also have substantial consequences. Forty percent of respondents said such an event would cause major operational disruption, while 18% anticipated significant financial loss or severe financial or reputational damage.

Another tension runs through Arelion's findings. Enterprises need networks they can trust to support AI projects, but many of the same decision-makers are concerned about AI's growing role inside those networks. A total of 54% of respondents identified AI and automation in network management and security as the factors most likely to negatively affect network trust over the next three years. That ranked ahead of data sovereignty expectations and geopolitical instability, both at 39%, and regulatory requirements and quantum computing threats to current encryption standards, both at 38%.

At the same time, respondents aren't rejecting AI as a network technology. Proactive AI-driven fault prediction was among the capabilities they said would matter most to future trust, alongside stronger built-in security, greater accountability, and responsive support. That leaves enterprises in an unusual position: AI is increasing their dependence on digital infrastructure while also raising questions about how that infrastructure itself is managed.

Arelion has an interest in emphasizing the importance of network-provider trust, and the survey reflects the views specifically of network decision-makers at large enterprises. Still, the findings point to an infrastructure consideration that can get lost amid the rush to adopt new AI models and agents. AI projects ultimately depend on the networks, cloud environments, data centers, and connectivity beneath them. If organizations lack confidence in that foundation, access to better AI models alone won't remove the obstacle.

"Trust is influencing which strategic initiatives move forward, how organizations manage risk, and which providers they choose to work with," said Mattias Fridström, Vice President at Arelion. "As businesses become increasingly reliant on digital infrastructure, enterprises are looking for network providers that can demonstrate transparency, accountability, and operational excellence, not just availability."

The full report is available for download here at the Arelion site (registration required)

#7072. Aneha Reveals She and Cam Both Planned to Say “No” at Their ‘Love Is Blind’ Wedding; Producers Encouraged Them to Go Anyway

Per Aneha, this was filmed almost *two* years ago.

Aneha and Cam were one of the few couples to actually make it to the altar during Love Is Blind season 11, and ultimately they said “I don’t.” Aneha was technically the one to turn Cam down with a “not yet,” but she just revealed they actually discussed it ahead of time and were both planning to say no. So, why’d they even go through with the wedding? Apparently, production encouraged them to—just in case they changed their minds at the last second.

“Cam and I had talked about it, and we said on both of our parts that most likely, it’s a no, but we should still go to the altar if there’s even the slightest chance that we say yes,” Aneha explained to Vulture. “Chris Coelen, the creator, talked to us about that: He said people, on the day, sometimes go from a no to a yes. And it’s worked out. That’s what we were hopeful for. We said regardless of what the answer was going to be, we’re going to stay together, whether married or engaged. But the day of it was tough because you put everything on, your family’s there, your friends are there, you’re celebrating this beautiful moment.”

Aneha added that “There was a feeling of disappointment mixed in with relief” when she turned Cam down, but that “I have to trust my gut. It didn’t feel right. I felt content, because we had already discussed that no matter what happens, we found each other through this amazing experience for a reason. We talked about being together in a more traditional relationship and seeing how that works. He could actually meet my family. They live in California, so it wasn’t the easiest thing for them to come meet Cam. They are shy and traditional in terms of how to marry.”

Love Is Blind season 11 was filmed almost two years ago, according to Aneha, and she and Cam are both currently single. “I felt like I almost let myself down,” she said. “I wish we had more time, maybe another month. I feel like I would have felt stronger in my opinion either way.”

The pair recently reunited during the reunion, so tune in on Sunday to find out where they stand.

#7071. Research: Lack of Specialized Infrastructure Is a Top AI Adoption Challenge

Enterprise AI projects are running into infrastructure questions as organizations move from planning toward deployment, according to recent research Digital Realty. Forty percent of survey respondents cited a lack of specialized infrastructure required by AI as a main challenge to adopting a formal AI strategy, up from 9% in the company's 2024 research.

The Global Data Insights Survey 2026 points to a widening mix of infrastructure models behind enterprise AI. Half of respondents host AI models in private clouds, 86% are pursuing or exploring sovereign AI, 92% tie data-location strategy to their AI plans, and 88% follow a distributed-data approach.

The research is based on an online survey of 2,131 IT decision-makers across 19 countries, and 11 industries, conducted from Nov. 23, 2025, through Feb. 4, 2026.

Organizations also appear to be shifting from AI planning toward execution. The share actively executing AI to achieve operational efficiency or reduce costs rose from 27% in 2024 to 37% this year. Only 3% reported no measurable ROI to date, while 63% expect returns within the next six months to two years.

Specialized infrastructure led the survey's list of AI adoption challenges at 40%, followed by regulatory and personal-data concerns at 35%. Data availability and quality, storage capacity, interconnection, compute resources, and staffing also ranked as significant constraints.

Those requirements are also shaping workload placement. Integration with existing IT or data infrastructure ranked first at 43%, while regulatory compliance and performance were each cited by 37%. Data proximity, sovereignty, sustainability, cost, and intellectual property protection also factored into hosting decisions.

Private cloud features prominently across the report's AI hosting categories, but enterprises are clearly using a mix of private cloud, public cloud, on-premises infrastructure, and colocation. Private cloud accounted for 50% of custom-built AI model hosting and 47% for open source or fine-tuned large language models (LLMs).

Sovereignty is another part of that placement decision. Thirty-two percent consider sovereign AI — in which data and AI models remain within a single national or regulatory jurisdiction — a requirement, while another 54% are exploring it. The findings point toward hybrid environments in which infrastructure location, governance and data control increasingly overlap.

#7070. The Best Mini PCs to Buy in 2026

Every product was carefully curated by an Esquire editor. We may earn a commission from these links. Here’s how we test products and why you should trust us.

These high-performing, small-footprint models will clean up your workspace.

Tiny computers are nothing new. I know you have one in your pocket right now—that is, if you’re not reading this on it already. The smartphone proved that computers can be anything, and anything can be a computer. Even putting aside the smartphone and the laptop, desktops have been getting smaller and changing form over time. The mini PC (sometimes also called a NUC) is a term that’s getting a lot of use lately, but it’s for something that’s more or less existed for decades: a small, prebuilt computer.

Traditionally, a PC runs Windows, but you can find Linux-based and MacOS-based mini PCs too. Of course, Apple makes its own in the form of the Mac Mini, probably the most popular one on the market. The original version debuted back in 2005, proving my point that these are nothing new, and has made its way into millions of homes without needing anyone to utter the words “mini PC.”

Point is, if you’re looking for something with the capability of a desktop without wanting a space-hogging tower, this is what you need to be looking at. Whether you need a machine for school, work, or gaming, these are the most capable desktops that come in shockingly small packages. These four machines are the best mini PCs to buy right now.

Starting off, it’s the 2026 Mac Mini. For the average person, you can start and stop the shopping journey here. If you want a compact home computer and you’re already deeply embedded in MacOS and Apple’s ecosystem, it’s hard to resist the simple appeal of the Mac Mini M6. It’s unassuming, smaller than a cable box and about the size of your internet modem. Ninety percent of the time, it’s as quiet as either of those too, despite being a fast mid-range computer optimized for AI workloads (more so than any generation before it). In terms of connections, you get a full stack; three Thunderbolt 4 ports, an HDMI port, and an Ethernet port in the back, as well as two USB-C ports and a headphone jack in front. More than plenty for all your accessories.

Like I said, this is a mid-range Mac. It’s just as suited for a home office as the main family computer in the living room. For something with professional level capabilities (we are all professionals, but I’m talking video editing, software development, any 3D model rendering), you’ll want the one with the M5 Pro chip instead. As always, with either of these, the biggest downside is Apple’s walled garden. You can’t upgrade the RAM or storage capacity like you can with other PCs, and Apple isn’t afraid to charge an arm and a leg for the versions that come with extra.

If you’re in both a time and money crunch, this is the kind of computer you want. For less than $400, it’s a Windows-capable machine with 16 gigs of RAM that can output to up to three 4K displays at a time. And unless you are pushing its processing power, it stays cool and relatively silent. It’s got all the ports you’d want—USB, HDMI, DPI, headphone jack—plus two high-speed USB-A ports in the back. These are decent for when you have large files to transfer, but they admittedly pale in terms of speed compared to a Thunderbolt port.

At the price it’s currently at, this is also cheaper than any gaming console out there. Not even as powerful as a Nintendo Switch (the old one), but it can run Minecraft and other casual or indie titles with little trouble.

If your job or home business requires running more and more local AI models (as many do), you might want a mini PC designed with these workflows in mind. Plus, it has every kind of port you’d want and more. With HDMI, USB, Thunderbolt, Ethernet, even an SD card slot and Copilot button, it’s fully kitted out for even the more niche uses.

It’s not a perfect solution. This machine will struggle with more intensive tasks and you’ll hear about it. For its size and light frame (it’s easily mountable to a monitor stand), though, it’s nothing to sneeze at.

Any machine can run some video games, but if you’re the type who considers gaming your hobby of choice, and you need something to play all the latest releases in 4K, well, then you need something as dedicated as you. For a reasonable price tag (trust me, it is), you’re getting a powerful PC with an RTX 5060 graphics card and 32 gigabytes of RAM. For the completely uninitiated, that’s a mid-range 2025 dedicated graphics card—sure to run any modern game, but when it comes to more demanding titles you just may have to choose to prioritize either frame rate or visuals.

Beyond gaming, this is a capable machine due to the decent GPU. And for those who don’t want to tinker at all, the G1 Pro offers three default modes to quickly swap between. Swap from Office to Gaming when it’s time to play, or go into Beast mode when you’re really trying to throttle the machine and get the most out of it.

The real reason to get something like this over a console or Valve’s Steam Machine is that it’s upgradeable. The storage and RAM can both be swapped out for newer or higher-capacity options. It’s not a full-on PC, though. With the CPU and GPU, you’re stuck with what is on board.

#7067. Hailey Bieber Went From Honey Bronde to Lacquer Brunette

Consider this your sign to get a new season hair refresh!

TikTok has a habit of turning the most niche beauty observations into full-fledged cultural phenomenons. One minute we're debating high contrast, low contrast makeup, the next we're all channeling “Tomato Girl Summer” or dabbing broccoli onto our faces in the pursuit of faux freckles (yes, it is I who did this in the name of boots-on-the-ground journalism). The app has become something of a crystal ball for beauty trends—that is, provided you're willing to sacrifice a few hours of your life to the algorithm.

But sometimes, it's worth looking up from your For You Page and remembering that celebrities have been setting beauty agendas long before TikTok got its hands on them. So, in the spirit of good old-fashioned beauty reporting, we took to the red carpet at the 2026 CFDA Fashion Awards (read: scrolled through pictures online from the comfort of our office desks) to see what the A-listers are wearing on their faces, and more importantly, doing with their hair this winter.

And at the front and center of the cultural zeitgeist? Hailey Bieber,, who arrived at the awards with a seasonal hair transformation that, of course, caught our eye. Enter: dark romance girl winter.

Gone are her familiar bronde lengths, and in their place comes a glossy, richer-toned shade of brown that looks almost lacquered. London-based hairstylist Marley Xavier previously dubbed this hair color “lacquer brunette,” predicting it would be a huge trend for the autumn/winter season. And, in our books, it's the unofficial hair hue of what we're calling the “dark romance girl” aesthetic. Think: moody autumn evenings, cashmere sweaters, burgundy nails, and vampy red lips.

Now, with Hailey being the face of said dark romance girl trend, it's likely we'll see an influx of blondes take to the at-home hair dye. But (!!) before you take the plunge, it's worth considering your natural contrast level—AKA, the difference in depth between your skin, hair, and eyes.

If, like Hailey, you have high-contrast coloring (where your features have a noticeable difference in depth), deeper shades like lacquer espresso and chocolate brunette can really complement your complexion. Those with softer, lower-contrast features, meanwhile, might find that more tonal shades, like mocha or mushroom brown, offer a more harmonious finish.

So, while you may want to make like Mrs. Bieber with a dark romance look for winter, it's worth considering the contrast of your features beforehand. Either that or you can go experimenting—after all, beauty is multifaceted; it’s functional while also playful, so do as you please!

Lia Mappoura (she/her) is the Beauty Writer at Cosmopolitan UK, with over four years of experience reporting across the brand's print, social, video and digital platforms. Lia covers everything from emerging trend analysis to viral celebrity hair and makeup moments, making her an expert at spotting the season’s next big beauty look (before it takes over social media feeds).

In 2025, she was named The Rising Media Star at the Love Perfume Awards with The Perfume Shop, recognised for her outstanding digital fragrance content and for building genuine authority within the space. She is passionate about challenging outdated beauty stereotypes, championing inclusive representation in beauty, and educating readers on the trends, products and conversations shaping the industry today. #7066. Please Just Tell Me Who the Next James Bond Is Already

Callum Turner, Jack Lowden, and Josh O’Connor are reportedly all up for the role. But I’m not having fun anymore.

Some movie announcements arrive as a complete surprise. Ryan Gosling as Ghost Rider? Very cool idea. Nathan Fielder secretly spending weeks staring into Elizabeth Holmes’s eyes for You Can See Everything? Amazing. In other instances, film studios will delay an announcement everyone knows is coming until their audience finds itself sucked up into the maelstrom and spun around until they’re so dizzy that they’re puking up that morning’s Cheerios. Maybe they wish they never even got excited in the first place.

That’s where I’m at with the current race for James Bond, which has reportedly come down to three actors: Jack Lowden (Slow Horses), Callum Turner (One Night Only), and Josh O’Connor (Disclosure Day). The three finalists were announced via Puck three weeks ago on October 22, along with a mysterious fourth actor whose identity has not yet been revealed, if he even exists.

Since then, there have been no official updates from Amazon/MGM—which acquired full control of the franchise in February 2025—but there have been dozens upon dozens of interviews from former Bond actors as well as anyone remotely associated with the prior 25 Bond films and novels, with each interviewee expressing that all three men up for the role would probably be great.

“All the men who are on the list now are actors I admire,” Pierce Brosnan recently told Vanity Fair. Maybe true but after a year of this guessing game, it just isn’t fun anymore. Even worse, no one seems concerned with even making it fun.

“It's an incredibly bizarre pinch-me moment... even being uttered in that same sentence and being considered for something like that,” Josh O’Connor told BBC News on Tuesday. “But I am afraid, there is nothing to say beyond that.”

Callum Turner was even more direct: “I’m not going to comment on it just because there’s no point in commenting on it in this moment,” he told Esquire UK in August.

Then, Lowden uttered “Oh, Christ” in an interview earlier this week when he finally addressed the rumors. “It’s all very flattering. It’s nice to be mentioned—for any role—and I’m just not really going to add to the noise.”

Are they not allowed to even say they want the role? Am I supposed to act excited when they finally announce if they don’t even seem excited themselves? Do we even want to see a film with Turner, Lowden, or O’Connor as James Bond?

Please add to the noise, guys. I’m begging you. It’s time to tell us.

#7065. OpenAI Intros Dots, AI Agents that Continue Working Between User Conversations

OpenAI has introduced Dots, GPT-6 Astra-powered agents that run on their own cloud computers, using connected applications to carry on with assignments between conversations and incorporating users' feedback as they go.

OpenAI is also previewing specialist Dots for organizations, with separate identities and credentials for defined responsibilities. Those enterprise deployments are beginning with focused pilots. The company is working with Microsoft on integration with Agent 365's governance and security controls.

The broader ambition seems to be to make delegation last beyond a chat session. That change could reduce the need to repeatedly explain a project. It also makes supervision a continuing obligation: An instruction that was appropriate yesterday may no longer fit today's circumstances.

Users currently cannot inspect or directly edit individual Dot memories. Deleting a Dot deletes its own context, while files and conversations it created remain separately stored. Plugin permissions are shared across Dots, ChatGPT, ChatGPT Work, and Codex.

For an organization, that creates two related governance questions: what an agent can retrieve now, and what it already knows. Revoking a connection addresses the first without necessarily resolving the second.

OpenAI's safety account distinguishes authorized tasks that continue in the background from unsolicited "proactive research." The latter uses tools restricted to reading permitted sources. Those research tasks cannot directly send messages, change connected application content, or control a browser or desktop. Any subsequent action must pass the usual rules and checks.

A separate system, Auto-review, checks certain planned actions against instructions and safety requirements. OpenAI says the agent cannot modify the controls enforcing those checks.

"Dots can still make mistakes," the company cautions.

OpenAI's Dots system-card appendix acknowledges that persistence and multi-agent systems are not new. Its evaluations examine how continuing work affects authorization boundaries.

In one test involving successive related tasks, moderate scope violations increased from 8.6% to 19.7% when the number of intervening tasks doubled from five to 10. Examples included carrying information between unrelated tasks or editing a shared document beyond the intended scope. The company reported no severe breaches or exfiltration in that evaluation.

These are controlled test results, not measured failure rates in customer deployments. They nevertheless identify a problem that a successful demonstration cannot settle: whether an agent keeps the right boundaries as its workload accumulates.

According to OpenAI's DevDay recap, Dots are available to Pro and Business Premium users in eligible markets. Enterprise, Edu, and Healthcare access is an administrator-enabled beta, off by default.

The operational question is how organizations will keep delegated authority current. A continuing assignment needs a human owner who can revise its scope and decide when to stop it. Otherwise, the convenience of leaving work with an agent can outlast the reason it was authorized.

For more information, go to the OpenAI site.
